Reactor settings fixture logs configured resolver timeout/cache size/thread-pool max, not measured timeout enforcement, global concurrency or identity separation. Multi fixture starts two empty crawlers: its log oracle cannot establish concurrent network work or separate accounts. Telnet-enabled reactorless fixture requests an unsupported extension and the parent test expects a warning; it does not exercise authenticated console access. No fixture was run and no package was installed.

Closed complete crawler tests (1156). Force-via-public-API tests replace reactor stop or use DummyLoop/DummyTask and assert callback scheduling/cancel invocation, not real process termination. Reactorless cleanup test has a real local test loop/thread and a coroutine converting cancellation to RuntimeError; its oracle checks exception-handler reporting, not a cancellation-resistant coroutine deadline. Thread helper joins without timeout. Late-component access tests enforce initialization timing and isinstance lookup, not caller permissions. Sequential/parallel second crawl attempts on the same Crawler are rejected; this is single-use object lifecycle, not job deduplication across crawlers or operators. This closes the initially identified lifecycle test dependency, while subprocess configuration fixtures and task/deferred utilities remain explicit unread material paths. No upstream test was executed here.

Follow-up closed subprocess test file (705), four sleeping fixtures (27/21/21/21) and command-line test helper (76). Actual forced-shutdown test sends three signals and asserts escalation log strings, then stop_spawn sends EOF and calls wait without its own timeout. SCRIPT_TIMEOUT bounds PopenSpawn expectations, not that final wait; generic run_script uses communicate without a timeout or return-code check. Thus the test comment “Guards against a hung subprocess” is narrower than a whole-process deadline oracle. Sleeping fixtures use data URLs and cooperative sleep/Deferred callbacks, not cancellation-resistant work. Graceful/fast cases assert shutdown log lines; these are useful lifecycle examples, not proof that arbitrary effects are rolled back or that a hung extension is terminated. Nothing in this follow-up was imported or run.

Completed crawler.py (1348), runner tests (271), signal implementation (34), deprecated wrapper (22), and signal tests (55). Source distinguishes per-crawler state from process-wide reactor, DNS resolver, thread pool, event loop and signal handlers. Conflicting reactor settings warn and use the first crawler; AsyncCrawlerProcess installs its loop early, before spider/add-on overrides. These are coordination choices, not account, device or tenant isolation. Runner tests establish configuration precedence, successful/failed bootstrap flags and two crawls completing with both a 200 response and exhausted 503 retries; join completion is not equivalent to every workload succeeding. These upstream tests were read, not executed here.

Shutdown has graceful, fast and force stages. Graceful/fast stop waits for join before stopping the reactor; the third signal stops the reactor or cancels the reactorless main task after a logging delay. Reactorless cleanup then cancels all tasks and gathers them before shutting down async generators and the default executor. There is no timeout around that gather/executor shutdown in this file: cancellation-resistant work can delay cleanup (source hypothesis, not a reproduced hang). Force therefore does not establish bounded termination or reversal of already completed downloads, writes or external effects. Old explanatory comments in _start_asyncio describe a second-signal kill, but actual handlers at 1306–1333 implement graceful, fast, then kill; enforcement code governs this finding.

The signal helper sets process-wide SIGTERM/SIGINT and Windows SIGBREAK handlers. Its default overrides an existing SIGINT handler; preservation is opt-in. Tests restore handlers in a fixture and assert installation/override behavior, without proving process shutdown deadlines. Async runner stop/join waits on tasks without checking every returned result in those methods; callers that need workload outcomes must retain and inspect crawl tasks. Cancellation and shutdown subprocess tests plus reactor/settings dependencies remain the next material review path; no runtime success is inferred from this source checkpoint.

## Mutable stats and default log disclosure

Completed stats collectors (188)/tests (248), action log formatter (195)/tests (308). get_stats returns the live dictionary, set_stats stores the supplied dictionary directly, and MemoryStatsCollector persists that same object by spider name. This is mutable in-process telemetry, not an immutable receipt, authenticated per-account ledger or distributed quota. DummyStatsCollector is deliberately truthy while reporting/storing nothing; a successful `if stats` check does not establish collection. Stats tests establish mapping/counter/deprecation behavior, and CoreStats uses mocked clock values for its exact snapshot. No unprovided concurrency or durable storage guarantee is inferred.

Default action formatter places whole scraped/dropped/error items and referrer/request descriptions into logs; tests assert those synthetic values appear. Plugin formatters may omit actions entirely, and exception/config can choose dropped-item severity, including values only rejected later by Python logging. Logging level, absence and parseable formatting are thus weak security outcomes. Formatter documentation/callers allow suppression; test values cover exact strings and configuration, not a redaction policy, signed attribution or privacy budget. No logs with real credentials or customer data were generated.

## DNS cache identity and shutdown fixture limits

Completed resolver (157)/tests (142), datatype implementation/wrapper/tests (131/24/404), eight resolver subprocess fixtures (37/26/22/32 for each process family). Global dnscache is keyed only by hostname, with no TTL, resolver identity, requested address family or transport-semantics key. Threaded resolver stores a string while hostname resolver stores address lists in the same global map. Switching resolver type with surviving entries therefore has incompatible-value risk; cached hostname results also ignore addressTypes/transportSemantics (source interactions, not reproduced locally). This is destination resolution/cache state, not worker egress allocation, IP rotation, device identity or operator attribution.

Disabling caching sets shared limit=0; LocalCache rejects future assignment but does not clear existing entries, and resolver reads still consult them. Tests reset the cache before every case and assert disabled-limit/miss behavior, so they do not establish invalidation of existing entries when disabling. Hostname cache hits copy port values without mutating cached entries, with direct IPv4/IPv6 oracles. HostResolution.cancel raises NotImplementedError; its registration as an interface is not cancellation enforcement. Threaded DNS timeout is explicitly supplied; hostname resolver delegates its miss path without that setting. Exact Twisted resolution behavior remains dependency work.

LocalCache is insertion-order bounded storage, not access-refreshed LRU, TTL, memory-byte budget or revocation list. Lowering limit does not immediately evict; existing-key updates skip eviction. Tests cover bounded insertion, zero from empty and weak-reference cleanup, not shrinking a live cache/negative limit. CaseInsensitiveDict copies key indexes independently but nested values remain shallow. SequenceExclude negates membership only, not an authorization policy.

Resolver fixtures include executable top-level process.start in deprecated-setting scripts, and IPv6 literal network requests or caller-supplied URLs in others. Their comments alone do not prove finite termination; caller subprocess deadlines/assertions must still be traced. None was imported or run, no resolver installed and no DNS query made. The eight files are actual complete reads, not credited from their similarity.

## Image processing and partial side effects

Completed ImagesPipeline (280)/full tests (776). Image decode/EXIF transpose/conversion/thumbnail generation run synchronously inside the generator before each awaited persist, without an explicit thread offload, maximum pixel count or processing deadline in this wrapper. Pillow may supply its own protections; exact decoder/version semantics are still unreviewed and must not be inferred absent or sufficient. Minimum dimensions reject small images, not maximum resource consumption. No image was opened/created/decoded locally in this review.

The full image is persisted before later thumbnail conversion/persistence, with no rollback across variants. A later thumbnail failure therefore does not imply no earlier stored image. First buffer checksum describes the full variant only. Original bytes are deliberately reused when target/source formats match and no resize occurs; tests assert that identity for JPEG and preserve-format PNG, so processing is not universally a metadata-strip/re-encode step. EXIF test inspects returned image pixels on a conversion path, not every original-byte reuse combination; do not infer universal sanitized/reoriented output bytes. URL hashes lack account/session identity and remain filenames rather than content integrity checks.

Settings tests establish independent modern per-instance file/image ACLs with unchanged class defaults, not provider-enforced permissions or isolation of legacy storage overrides. The whole test module skips if Pillow or JPEG codec support is absent; the missing-Pillow test is itself inside that module-level availability condition. No test execution count or available-codec coverage was claimed. Synthetic image vectors establish selected colors, aspect ratio, path and encoding, not hostile-file detection accuracy, privacy cleansing or bounded runtime memory.

## FilesPipeline implementation checkpoint; full tests next

Full FilesPipeline tests (1799) now read. Freshness tests explicitly accept checksum=None, retry expired/missing-time entries and permit negative expiry to retain indefinitely. Mock download tests assert downloaded/cached status and local file bytes, configurable SHA256 and rejected unsupported algorithm; they do not compare to an authenticated publisher digest. Custom item/response-based file_path tests establish hook flexibility with benign paths, not confinement. Filtered IgnoreRequest yields no stored results and debug-only logs. List-type validation does not validate each destination against an authorization policy.

S3 Stubber confirms x-amz-acl header overrides configured ACL to public-read (1156–1188); fixed default private policy is thus not an immutable enforcement boundary. Async tests establish sequential reuse, close reset and repeated-close no-op, not concurrent create/close fencing. GCS tests explicitly construct with missing get/create permissions and check logs, confirming diagnostic-only admission. Modern from_crawler tests assert class defaults unchanged, qualifying the legacy class-mutation concern to STORE_SCHEMES paths. Local/FTP storage tests cover ordinary persistence/checksum/stat errors; no whole-file cancellation, path-escape or concurrent writer oracle appears. Optional cloud test skips and configured pool/timeout values are not live integration results. All reviewed tests stayed unexecuted.

Full FilesPipeline/storage implementation (980) reviewed; its 1799-line test file is still unread and must be traced next before closing this graph. Filesystem store joins configured base with supplied path components and writes directly, without a resolved-path containment check, exclusive writer lease or atomic replace. Default pipeline paths are URL-hash-derived; custom file_path hooks/configured paths remain trusted, so no hostile remote traversal reachability is asserted. Neither URL hash nor file checksum authenticates an operator or file publisher.

Expiry compares current wall time with stored modification time; missing/failed stats force download, and an unexpired entry may return checksum=None after a once-only warning. That is freshness optimization, not integrity verification against an expected trusted digest. Stat errors, including permissions/transport failures, are broadly converted to download-needed; FTP stat catches any Exception as empty and does not pass UPLOAD_TIMEOUT to its connect. GCS constructor calls test_iam_permissions and logs missing create permission without refusing construction; constructor inspection is therefore not a universally inert runtime operation. No store was instantiated here.

Modern storage configuration is passed per instance, but deprecated STORE_SCHEMES overrides write configuration onto storage class attributes, potentially affecting other instances using those classes (source interaction, tests pending). S3 async client is lazily shared and closed through FilesPipeline.close_spider; no closed latch or lock in close fences concurrent lazy creation. S3/GCS media writes are ordinary overwrite operations here, unlike feed create modes. S3 AWS_VERIFY/AWS_USE_SSL are configured, not immutable protection. MD5/ETag availability comments do not prove cryptographic provenance or every provider's semantics. Full tests and exact SDK dependencies remain outstanding; no external traffic, IAM query, credentials, files or upload were exercised.

## Media coalescing, cached failures and cancellation questions

Completed media pipeline (347), full tests (671), mock helper (22). Per-spider fingerprint cache coalesces requests across items and stores successes and failures; tests explicitly expect later request metadata responses to be ignored for equal fingerprints. With the default fingerprinter this does not add account/session/cookiejar identity. Cached FileInfo objects are returned directly and caller errbacks can recover a cached failure. It is not a tenant-isolation boundary. LRU limits completed entries only; zero disables retained completed cache, negative means unbounded, while in-flight sets/waiter lists are separate. process_item eagerly builds all request coroutines and gathers them; outer downloader budgets remain distinct from queued task memory and this cache limit.

media_failed returning None is explicitly tested as (True, None), so the success boolean alone does not prove a downloaded file or valid FileInfo. Default item_completed returns the item even after failures, optionally logging item data; filtered requests deliberately produce no error log. Lack of an error log or item completion is not a delivery/security oracle. Redirect flags control downloader status handling, not destination consent or independent origin authorization.

The first pending-I/O await after adding the in-flight fingerprint is before the try/except, and the completion/waiter cleanup is not in finally; asyncio cancellation uses BaseException rather than the caught Exception path. Cancellation at those points may leave in-flight/waiter bookkeeping stranded (source hypothesis requiring a bounded runtime oracle, not reproduced here). Canceling a waiter also needs callback behavior traced through Twisted before asserting complete fan-out cleanup. Existing tests inspect two coalesced waiters and expected result reuse but contain no cancellation case. Failure cleanup test constructs StopIteration context and asserts its removal; its own comment explicitly says it does not detect every possible retained-reference leak. No downloader or upstream coroutine was executed here.

## Export projection, nested data and byte-level test limits

Completed exporters (456)/full tests (754), Item (155)/tests (346). Top-level fields_to_export is not recursive: exporter tests 169–176 explicitly require a nested item's name to remain even when only age is selected at the top level. Field metadata may execute trusted serializers, including on nested items. This is serialization/projection, not a recursive secret-removal policy. Item restricts declared key names but accepts arbitrary values without schema/type/permission validation; repr prints all populated values, consistent with the earlier diagnostic path. copy is shallow; deepcopy has an explicit nested-list separation test. Neither operation creates a tenant principal.

JSON exporter fully encodes a record before writing its comma/data; tests inject serialization and encoding errors between two good records and assert valid output containing just those good records. This is meaningful per-record failure evidence, not rollback after a partial filesystem write. XML/CSV detach their text wrapper only on finish; test comments acknowledge deletion can otherwise close the underlying file. CSV auto-detected headers can drop later fields and warn once; no spreadsheet-formula neutralization is present in this wrapper. XML text goes through XMLGenerator while field names become element names; exact name validation remains a dependency/application contract, not proven by data escaping.

Several CSV test comparisons sort regex-split tokens within each line, so they do not independently prove column order or exact bytes. XML equivalence recursively sorts children, weakening sibling-order evidence. Separate feed exact-byte tests support selected ordering/encoding cases; no universal ordering claim follows. Pickle/marshal tests decode only locally generated synthetic outputs; their format availability does not make arbitrary received blobs safe. The Pprint test uses eval on its own generated fixture, not a production evaluation path. ItemAdapter, standard serializers and XMLGenerator exact implementations remain material dependencies; no new repository was added and no exporter was run here.

## Feed flow checkpoint: postprocessing and storage semantics

Remaining inventoried feed test files closed: filters/processors 207, init/signals 239, fields 238, formats 328, plus cloud autospec helper29. Filters test exact per-feed payloads and source item vs expanded output counters (one scraped item, two exported records), not independent tenant labels. Empty FEED_EXPORT_FIELDS is explicitly treated as default/all fields rather than export-none. CSV derives initial headers differently from JSON Lines; helper parsing drops empty CSV fields, so parsed-row comparisons intentionally lose missing-vs-empty distinction. Exact byte-format tests separately cover encoding/indent choices. Signals tests use booleans for receipt, not exact count/order/durable success; their named temporary output setup occurs at class-definition time, another reason to avoid blanket importing test modules as inert inventory. Cloud autospec mocks match API attributes but contain no remote state model or concurrency oracle. Exporter serialization/ItemAdapter implementations remain material dependencies to trace next; finishing these test files does not equate to complete end-to-end dependency understanding.

Full storage tests added (1081). Local file/loopback FTP tests check append/overwrite/sequential create rejection and FTP_TLS trusted/untrusted certificate outcomes; they do not exercise concurrent create races or unsupported SIZE. Cloud tests assert supplied credential precedence, client config values, conditional request parameters and exception mapping using mocks/stubs. S3 async error variants check file.closed; GCS upload/compose failures check close and temporary-part delete on compose error. Part upload occurs before the compose-finally region, so upload failure after server-side creation is not covered by that cleanup oracle; delete failure can also replace the compose exception (source paths, untested locally). GCS absent-target append uses an ordinary upload without generation-match, unlike explicit create, so a competing creator is not fenced in this wrapper. No claim of measured cloud corruption or real permissions follows.

Pool-size and upload timeout tests assert configuration only: S3 UPLOAD_TIMEOUT maps to read_timeout (default client 60), not a whole-upload deadline or thread cancellation. URI credentials override settings; this is configuration precedence, not automatic least-privilege account provisioning. Stdout accepts all three modes and deliberately ignores their storage semantics. Cloud success tests do not attest persistence beyond the mocked SDK call. Exact cloud SDK/native TLS internals and real provider integrations remain runtime/dependency limitations, not blockers to remaining source review.

Batch/URI tests added (534/291). Batch delivery is checked during a later callback, with exact first-batch JSON before crawl close; complete per-format vectors check batch contents and per-feed settings. JOBDIR resume test performs two orderly runs and checks preserved/new files; it does not simulate crash between delivery and state persistence, so exactly-once resume is not established. Filters/processors can expand one item into several batch-counted outputs. S3 batch tests use botocore Stubber or AsyncMock, synthetic keys and success counters, not live S3 or credential-scope validation; neither establishes maximum simultaneous stores. URI tests verify literal percent encoding, placeholders, custom parameter override and missing-placeholder slot omission. Trusted callback returning an empty dict causes skipped feed, while returning None keeps default attributes. Those are configuration outcomes, not guaranteed requested output completion.

Async/mode/empty test closure added (231/317/100). Async CallTracker resets shared counters, suspends inside each tracked component and checks max_active==1 for the single-feed non-batching case; this meaningfully tests item-component serialization, not all batch storage concurrency. It asserts exact call counts and sorted item content, deliberately ignoring completion order. No cancellation/error variant is in that file. Create-conflict tests explicitly permit finish_reason='finished' even when every feed target exists and no success counter appears; later batches continue after a conflict. Thus crawl completion cannot be the delivery oracle. DelayedFileStorage tests simulate delayed conflict checking with local exists/write operations and do not establish cloud-provider atomicity. Unknown legacy storage mode support only warns, confirmed by tests. Empty-feed tests establish expected absent/empty-format output and no store callback when disabled, not successful effect cleanup after an earlier open failure.

Lifecycle/reporting closure added: complete files 172/150. Export-item exception tests assert the full item repr appears in error logs, confirming the diagnostic disclosure path for synthetic item data. Instrumented start/finish listener stores booleans, so it can establish final pairing state but not an exact number of invocations or independent file-descriptor cleanup. Its injected exporter fails export_item, not constructor/start/finish; those failure stages remain unproven by this test. Reporting tests assert separate success/failed counters and stored/error messages for synchronous and blocking synthetic storages, supporting the need to inspect outcomes rather than infer success from close completion. No upload durability or provider permissions are established.

Completed periodic logger (185)/tests (267), postprocessing (164)/full tests (589), feed test helper (164), full feed exporter implementation (1140), FTP upload helper (84). Broader feed lifecycle/batch/storage/failure tests remain explicit unread work; this implementation checkpoint does not close that graph. No exports/uploads, compression or pickle decoding were run here.

Periodic logging filters by stat-name substring, with excludes winning, and defaults to all stats when no filters exist. Nonempty configuration dictionaries enable regardless of an internal enabled=False key because bool(dict) is used. Numeric deltas include bool and compute from zero for new/reappearing keys. Tests assert selected key lists but do not assert numeric delta values. Timing uses UTC wall-clock differences, not monotonic. Logging on close precedes task.stop, so serialization failure can prevent stopping that task in this method (source interaction, untested here). Include/exclude is observability selection, not secret classification or access control.

PostProcessingManager nests trusted plugin constructors in declared order and closes only the head, depending on plugins to propagate closure. Built-in compression wrappers finalize their codec stream while underlying storage ownership is handled by FeedExporter._get_file and storage.store; a direct test asserts the same underlying file reaches store and remains open until then. Default compressed-validity tests only decompress without checking expected payload, while parameterized and composition tests do assert exact output or decoded bytes. Compression checks are not message authentication. Constructor/write/finalization failure cleanup remains a material path to compare with unread lifecycle/storage tests.

Feed export serializes item processing using one in-process DeferredLock, then schedules batch storage asynchronously; multiple pending stores have no explicit independent concurrency cap here. Store failures are logged/counted, and feed_slot_closed is emitted after caught failures too, so the signal alone does not mean durable success. Finish/start exceptions occur before the storage try block. Feed URI parameters enumerate spider attributes and support trusted callback overrides; logs can include resolved URIs and full failing item repr, without this code providing a secret-redaction boundary. Item class filters default allow-all and are not tenant permissions.

Create modes differ materially: local file uses exclusive xb; S3 passes IfNoneMatch='*'; GCS passes generation-match zero; FTP checks SIZE then STOR and explicitly documents best-effort creation. FTP permission/unsupported SIZE errors are treated as absence, not enforced no-overwrite protection. Append on GCS uploads a temporary part and composes named sources without a generation fence in this wrapper; retries/concurrent append effects need remaining tests and exact cloud SDK semantics before stronger claims. Source registration or declared supported_modes is not proof of uniform enforcement. FTP_TLS uses default certificate context and protected data channel in the helper; dependency internals remain outside this read. No network control or credentials were exercised.

## Log attribution, counters and time-derived rates

Completed core stats (59), log counting (46), periodic rates (97)/tests (97), logging helpers (374)/tests (577), color detection (48), version helper (40). CoreStats uses monotonic elapsed duration with separate UTC timestamps. LogStats final rate instead uses datetime delta.seconds, dropping days and fractional seconds; periodic rates multiply observed deltas by the configured interval rather than measuring actual elapsed tick time. Its tests cover 30-minute and zero-duration cases, not multi-day/subsecond/clock-change cases. These telemetry values should not serve as authoritative rate-budget enforcement.

LogCount attaches a handler to the process root logger; LogCounterHandler increments every accepted record's level without checking record.crawler/spider. Concurrent crawler handlers can therefore count one another's root-propagated messages (source path, not a locally reproduced concurrency result). LOG_LEVEL filtering itself has direct tests. SpiderLoggerAdapter overwrites supplied spider extras with its configured object, as tests require, but no cryptographic attribution follows. Default placeholder '-' fills missing context, not a resolved principal. Top-level formatting mutates logger names and reduces detail.

Logging configuration changes process-global levels, warning routing, stdout and Twisted observers. Tests explicitly restore observer lists because configure_logging offers no removal handle; root-handler replacement closes only its tracked handler. Custom formatters are trusted imports. Plain logging and the JSON-looking test format interpolate strings rather than proving structured escaping, redaction or tamper evidence. Color capability/platform detection and version reporting are presentation/diagnostics, not authentication; on supported Windows builds color detection calls SetConsoleMode, so even that helper is not universally read-only. No global logging/environment or console-mode changes were executed here.

## Lag warnings and diagnostic signal ownership

Completed debug extensions (76)/tests (139) and event-loop lag monitor (71)/tests (59). Lag samples monotonic tick delay minus expected interval and only logs when above threshold; zero disables. It neither identifies a responsible principal nor preempts the blocking work, and the warning's CPU-callback attribution is a hypothesis rather than measured causation. Moving work to a thread is advice, not enforced cancellation or isolation. The lag test actually blocks with time.sleep for 0.2/0.8 seconds at a 0.5-second threshold and asserts no warning/one warning with parsed lag 0.6–0.9; this is an upstream bounded timing oracle, not a local measured result or proof of preemption during blocking.

Debug extensions install process-global signal handlers when their platform supports them, with no handler restoration in this file. StackTraceDump and Debugger both use SIGUSR2, so last installation determines that signal's behavior; this is not independent per-crawler control. Dump enumerates all process thread stacks and engine/live-object diagnostics; Debugger enters Pdb. Tests restore real handlers in a fixture, check installed handlers, inspect real thread stacks, and include a POSIX-only self-signal crawl oracle; Pdb entry is mocked. They do not prove access control or confidentiality of dumps. No signals were registered or sent and no debugger was entered during this review.

## Memory thresholds are sampled cooperative controls

Completed memory usage (184)/tests (209), memory debug (40)/tests (51), engine-status helper (54). Despite get_virtual_size naming, this pin reads process peak RSS (resource.ru_maxrss) or Windows psutil peak_wset, not current virtual size or fleet/child-process totals. It periodically checks a strict greater-than limit and schedules orderly spider/crawler closure, not an OS-enforced allocation cap. Prior peaks persist; a later crawler can encounter an already exceeded process peak. Warning is once-only and nondisruptive. Scheduling checks cannot preempt a blocked loop or constrain allocation between samples.

The entire memory-usage test module has a Windows skip marker even though source now includes a psutil fallback. Its fallback oracle mocks peak_wset, and threshold tests mock size and looping calls; they establish routing/stat/log behavior, not a measured hard memory bound or Windows integration. Notification mail paths are marked no-cover; none was enabled or sent. The implementation does not become a hard kill switch because its log says shutting down.

MemoryDebugger collects GC and reports opted-in live references on close; same class-name stat keys can overwrite each other. Engine status evaluates a fixed internal list of expressions, catches exceptions as exception-type strings, and is a sequential snapshot, not atomic distributed state or user-supplied eval input. No matching dedicated utils-engine test file exists in the pinned main inventory; callers/tests may exercise portions indirectly. No process resource experiment, GC invocation or mail execution was performed here.

## Closing thresholds and persisted job-state ownership

Completed CloseSpider (148), tests (115), asyncio no-item fixture (38), SpiderState (95), tests (96), config helpers (232), tests (270). CloseSpider observes signals/counters and schedules close_spider_async; it does not synchronously revoke credentials, kill processes or undo effects. Item/page/error threshold tests intentionally permit counts >= threshold, and timeout tests establish a lower elapsed bound without an upper termination deadline. No-item timeout uses fixed periodic windows, not a continuously reset deadline from the last item. The asyncio fixture emits 30 items on delays but contains no inline assertion itself; its expected success must be tied to its caller before treating it as an independent test result.

SpiderState loads pickle at opening and writes directly to JOBDIR/spider.state on orderly close after saving signals. There is no temp-file rename, fsync, version/fencing token, signature, secret encryption, cross-process lease or per-account namespace here. _job_dir only creates a configured missing directory; it neither reserves it nor verifies exclusive job ownership. Two crawlers using the same directory deliberately share state in the round-trip test, not isolated tenants. Abrupt interruption/concurrent writes/corrupt pickle recovery are not tested by this file. State files therefore remain trusted persistence, and orderly round trip is not crash durability or credential isolation.

Config component priorities select/sort enabled plugins and validate numeric values/duplicate converted names, not plugin authority. Config discovery walks parent paths and user/system config locations; init_env can append the discovered project directory to sys.path. Feed options merge settings over CLI entries; format validation says nothing about destination authorization or safe overwrite. These are inspected configuration behaviors, with no local environment change, file-store experiment or feed upload performed.

## Loader and callback-diagnostic dependencies

Completed misc utilities (312), tests (177), generator-warning tests (282) and four empty module-walk fixture files. load_object imports an arbitrary configured module and returns its named attribute; a direct callable is returned unchanged. walk_modules_iter imports recursively while enumerating. build_from_crawler delegates to the plugin's from_crawler or constructor, rejecting only a None result. These helpers contain no provenance signature, allowed-module policy, sandbox or capability reduction. Existing persisted _class and settings paths therefore require trusted configuration/storage; import failure validation is not authentication.

set_environ mutates the process-wide environment and restores named values in finally; it does not provide concurrent per-worker secret isolation. Its tests cover serial normal restoration only. Callback-return diagnosis inspects source AST, skips nested functions and warns; it is disabled by setting and cannot establish safe execution or termination. The warning helper catches IndentationError specifically, not every source-inspection failure. Tests deliberately distinguish generator/non-generator wrappers and nonrecursive inspection. Empty module fixtures carry no behavior, and the test.egg contents remain an exact dependency not credited by the module-name assertions. No imports, environment mutation or plugin execution were performed in this review.

## Live-object and downloader-exit observability limits

Completed trackref (91), tests (101), request-left signal tests (65). Tracking holds weak maps of opted-in class instances with monotonic creation time, supporting counts and oldest object inspection. It does not enumerate all Python/native memory, external processes, outstanding effects, sockets or credentials. Class-name lookup returns the first matching class, so same-name classes are not independently addressable by this API. Dynamic-class cleanup test proves weak-reference behavior under its GC helper, not bounded whole-process memory or measured overhead; the module's minimal-impact claim was not benchmarked here.

Downloader-exit tests assert exactly one signal for success, timeout, dropped connection and connection failure. They do not assert effect rollback or successful remote cancellation. The no-connect test uses an external DNS hostname without an internet marker in this file; do not run the whole file as a loopback-only lab. No such DNS query or test was executed. Signal accounting and worker liveness remain distinct from revocation and cleanup guarantees.

## Curl text conversion and fingerprint oracles

Completed curl parser (190), full parser tests (370) and request utility tests (546); reread fingerprint implementation 1–179 and command rendering 247–282. curl_to_request_kwargs uses shlex/argparse, not shell execution, and never runs the command. It imports explicit auth/cookie/header text into a request; cookie filenames are ignored, unsupported options warn by default, and compressed handling relies on middleware defaults. Normalizing Bash ANSI-C quote prefixes is not full Bash escape evaluation. The fixtures test parsed dictionaries and construct Requests, without contacting fixture URLs.

The reverse request_to_curl rendering inserts body/header/cookie values inside single quotes without escaping embedded quotes, leaves URL unquoted and collapses all whitespace in the finished string. It is consequently a display convenience, not a shell-safe serializer or a guaranteed byte-preserving replay artifact. Verbose-cookie tests deliberately render only name/value, dropping domain/path/secure attributes; that output does not preserve session scope. Current tests do not include embedded quote or significant repeated-whitespace round trips. This source finding does not imply this helper executes hostile input. Commands containing real credentials must not be treated as redacted receipts.

Fingerprint tests explicitly expect SESSIONID and other headers to be ignored by default; selected-header inclusion is optional and separate from cookiejar/account/tenant metadata. Handler ID and verbatim_url affect cache keys, with direct mutation tests for handler ID. The per-request fingerprint cache key omits header values and mutable method; mutating included headers or method after caching can retain an earlier digest for the same options (source interaction hypothesis; no dedicated runtime reproduction here). Fresh request replacement has a tested new-cache oracle. Fixed known hashes establish compatibility for selected vectors, not cryptographic authentication, account separation or universal resource equivalence. These points strengthen the earlier cross-account cache warning without claiming a measured customer leak.

## Form construction is not an account-assignment boundary

Completed FormRequest (329) and its full tests (1071), including inherited request base already reviewed. Direct construction URL-encodes truthy formdata; GET/non-POST paths replace the query, not merge it, with an explicit test proving removal of earlier keys. HTML from_response is deprecated at this pin, while direct construction is not. No replacement form2request dependency was fetched or credited.

HTML form action resolves against the parsed document base URL and may cross origins: test 963–979 explicitly expects a.com to produce b.com/test_form. Missing form name/id falls back to another selector or form number, including the default first form; tests 637–709 expect that behavior. This is convenient extraction, not a fail-closed form identity or credential destination check. Calling applications must bind approved destination and synthetic account separately. Hidden controls are copied as data; that is not token verification, consent or session provenance.

Default clicking chooses the first matching control; dont_click suppresses that addition, not request execution. Explicit fields override/remove controls; None removes fields and duplicate tuples preserve multiple values. Selector names and click criteria interpolate into XPath, with no quoting in this wrapper, so those configuration arguments remain trusted (no hostile-input reachability claimed). nr indexes form.inputs rather than an independently authenticated clickable. Unknown HTML form methods normalize to GET; explicit None yields the literal method NONE in a direct test, while direct constructor accepts inherited methods. Tests check synthetic extracted values, encodings, errors and chosen URLs, not browser behavioral equivalence, account isolation or successful remote submissions. No requests were sent.

## Request binding and attribution test closure

Completed JSON request (80)/tests (189), XML-RPC wrapper (43)/request tests (43), and inherited request test base (534). JSON request deep-copies serializer options, defaults sort_keys, and serializes supplied data with standard json.dumps; explicit body wins with warning, even empty body, and data=None means absent. Merely setting Content-Type does not validate an explicit body. replace(data=...) serializes using the original object's options before constructing the replacement, so supplying replacement dumps_kwargs does not necessarily govern that same operation's serialization (source-derived interaction, no local reproduction). No canonical signing or schema guarantee is provided.

XML-RPC import applies a process-wide defusedxml monkey patch; its exact dependency implementation remains unreviewed. POST and dont_filter are defaults enforced through setdefault, so caller overrides remain possible; dont_filter is a duplicate-filter switch, not rate-limit or authorization exemption. Tests compare serializer output, encoding and default method only, with synthetic strings. Inherited base tests cover fresh copy IDs and configurable parent IDs, shallow state copying, arbitrary accepted schemes and verbatim_url normalization bypass. Request construction is not a network destination allowlist. Curl conversion fixtures parse text without contacting the displayed destinations, and ignoring unknown options by default is explicit tested behavior.

Completed attribute-binding (234), request-dictionary (264), callback-keyword (191), and lineage (67) tests, following the previously reviewed request/scraper implementation. Attribute-binding tests explicitly expect trusted downloader middleware to replace response.request: signals and the crawled log then carry the substituted request URL, and the spider uses its alternate callback and cb_kwargs. Scraper 349–352 selects those fields from result.request. Consequently these logs and parent identifiers describe application-managed request objects; they do not independently attest the actual wire destination, account principal or policy authorizer. Synthetic middleware is trusted application code, not an established remote attacker path.

Serialization tests retain id/parent_id, cookies, metadata and callback arguments; absent id generates another identifier. Private/mixin/delegated callbacks can round-trip by method name, while lambdas, removed methods and unresolved methods raise ValueError. This is resolvability, not a permitted-callback capability list. Lineage oracle covers redirect, retry, errback output and explicit parent override, establishing attribution behavior without making those IDs security identities.

Callback-argument tests intentionally permit downloader and spider middleware mutation. Their checks list is a class attribute and the final length compares to an accumulated counter, without a fixed expected total; this is weaker than an independent clean-state, exact-callback-coverage oracle. These upstream tests were reviewed, not executed. No account/session isolation measurement is inferred from them.

## Response identity, decoding and trusted reconstruction

Additional closure: selector wrapper/initializer (133/12), complete selector tests (278), response-dictionary tests (113), JSON encoder/tests (29/96) reviewed. Selector delegates parsing to parsel, selecting XML/JSON/HTML from response class and leaving plain TextResponse detection to the dependency. The byte-body fast path requires parsel >=1.12.0, nonempty body and HTML/XML type; otherwise it uses decoded text. Tests assert malformed HTML repair differs by XML/HTML mode and that JsonResponse can retain JSON selector type even for an HTML body. These are tolerant extraction semantics, not a content-validation boundary. Tests include a badly encoded body with no assertion beyond successful extraction, so that test is weaker than a byte-preservation oracle.

Response-dictionary tests explicitly show request and certificate are discarded, headers lists copied, and plugin-defined class/instance attributes reconstructed. Production caller search identifies the two HTTP-cache storage retrieval paths (httpcache.py 292/364), not a generic remote API endpoint. Both load persisted pickle first; the existing trusted-cache boundary remains the prerequisite, rather than claiming a newly demonstrated remote exploit. ScrapyJSONEncoder renders requests/responses as strings containing URL/method/status, not redacted audit records or round-trip workload identity. No credentials or customer data were used.

Completed response base (388), body-file (41), JSON/HTML/XML markers (13/14/14), type selection (138), text response (343), headers (233), response utilities (152), and their complete reviewed test files (22/9/164/451/740/236/371). These are source/test-oracle reviews, with no upstream package execution or browser opening.

Response.meta and cb_kwargs expose the originating Request dictionaries directly; base tests 96–110 assert that identity. copy/replace retain the request reference, while constructor copying creates independent flag lists and Headers value lists. Headers tests 149–154 explicitly check separate lists; getlist returns the live stored list, and assigning an existing Headers object to response.headers preserves its identity (base tests 216–221). These are data-sharing contracts, not tenant or account boundaries. follow constructs a new request from explicit arguments; follow_all shallow copies dictionaries but mutable values remain shared. parent_id is attribution, not an authenticated operator identity.

Text decoding chooses explicit encoding, then BOM, HTTP declaration and body declaration, with ASCII/UTF-8 attempts and charset-normalizer fallback. Tests deliberately accept replacement characters for malformed UTF-8 and latin-1 resolution to cp1252. json() first parses bytes, falling back to decoded text only on UnicodeDecodeError, then caches the returned object, including null. Non-UTF8 JSON acceptance and misleading charset handling have direct test oracles. Response class markers and MIME/body guessing therefore do not prove syntax, schema, safe content or byte validity; external w3lib/charset-normalizer/parsel implementations remain dependency review work.

TextResponse.urljoin and follow honor a document base URL, which may point to another origin (tests 320–341); manual follow_redirect instead resolves Location against the response URL (343–353) and defaults to GET, rather than inheriting automatic redirect middleware semantics. Neither helper is an origin authorization check. Base URL/meta-refresh parsing examines the first 4096 decoded characters and caches per response; the meta-refresh cache does not include ignore_tags in its key, so different later arguments reuse the first result (source hypothesis, no dedicated runtime reproduction here).

Response serialization omits the live request/certificate; reconstruction through response_from_dict accepts a _class import path and invokes load_object before from_dict. This is trusted persistence/plugin loading, not an authenticated interchange format; reachability from hostile persisted data must be established separately. BodyFileResponse stores a path without establishing ownership, confinement or deletion policy. open_in_browser writes content to a temporary file and invokes the supplied browser function; its tests substitute a local callback, so they do not establish browser sandboxing, absence of subresource traffic or cleanup. Reviewed CPU-budget regression oracles concern earlier regex behavior only and were not run here.

## Decompression enforcement and partial acceptance

Completed compression middleware/helper/deprecated wrapper (207/139/17), helper tests (64), full middleware tests (932), and benchmark source (173). Decode order unwinds supported outer encodings until an unknown layer, retaining unknown encodings and warning rather than universally rejecting. HEAD and BodyFileResponse skip decoding. Per-request limits override defaults; zero disables the output-byte check. Each layer checks accumulated produced bytes before appending its next chunk to the output buffer, with a requested 64 KiB decoder chunk. This bounds accepted per-layer output with chunk overshoot, not total process memory, CPU time, native decoder working memory or aggregate concurrent work.

Gzip intentionally returns already produced data after selected CRC/truncation errors; Brotli can return output when no further chunk is produced despite not being finished; Zstd catches EOF and may return empty. Tests explicitly accept partial prefix output for gzip/deflate/Brotli and empty output for truncated Zstd. A successfully returned response is therefore not a proof of an intact compressed representation, and helper acceptance need not match another security parser. Invalid initial compressed content has separate error tests. Full binary fixtures and native/external codec implementations remain exact dependencies; file names/comments are not credited as their semantic review. No compressed fixture was decoded or high-expansion payload generated/run here.

Size-limit tests assert rejection with a typed cause and produced size below 1.1 MB for a 1 MB limit across four codecs and setting/spider/request channels. Warn tests instead allow the larger output and assert a warning; an explicit request max disables the default warn threshold. These are meaningful reviewed oracles, not measured memory/cpu/runtime results from this session. A trailing-deflate-data regression uses a daemon thread and 60-second join to detect nontermination; it does not forcibly terminate the thread.

Bodies above the compressed-size threshold dispatch through run_in_thread; task cancellation is not proof of native decoding preemption. Benchmark returns synthetic responses through an in-process handler and checks 32 decoded-response counters. Its handler body has no await and its peak-active counter wraps response construction only, not concurrent decompression; no assertion establishes the claimed degree of overlapping decoder work. Benchmark comments about tuned speed are upstream claims, with no local timing or whole-system memory result. No crawler, codec package or network request was executed.


## Header defaults, timeout hints and observed counters

Completed default-header source/tests (38/36), timeout source/tests (42/53), JSON validation source/tests (48/85), stats source/tests (79/96), user-agent source/tests (41/39), and empty package initializer. Header/User-Agent and timeout middleware use setdefault, respecting explicit request values rather than authenticating or forcing them. Timeout zero omits the metadata field in its direct test, not a proof that every downstream transport disables timeouts; handler fallbacks already differ. User-Agent remains a caller-controlled claim, not proof of a legitimate bot.

JSON validation is opt-in and applies only to JsonResponse, non-HEAD successful non-204 responses. It checks syntax through response.json, not schema, consent, authorization or semantic safety; other response classes/statuses pass. Its manager test checks a retry request only with response-exception handling enabled, and decompression/type conversion precedes validation in another oracle. This is retry policy, not a signature or account validation path.

Downloader counters are middleware observations and reconstruct an HTTP/1.1-style message size from status/header/body lengths; tests match that representation including duplicate headers and body files. They do not measure HTTP/2 compressed frames, TLS/proxy overhead or bytes on the wire, and counters do not enforce quotas. Full compression and response parsing dependencies remain to inspect before resource-limit claims. No tests, decoders, network clients or account actions were executed.


## Non-HTTP capabilities and delegated cleanup

Completed file/data/S3/Twisted-FTP/async-FTP handlers (23/25/84/208/281), boto helpers (31), combined handler tests (633), Twisted FTP tests (321) and async FTP tests (442). File handler reads a supplied local path fully through a thread helper, without an internal root/byte cap; data URI parsing decodes supplied bytes without the HTTP streaming quota path. Enabling these handlers grants additional process capabilities, not merely HTTP transport selection, and must not be confused with a network-only crawler sandbox. No file or network handler was executed.

S3 captures configured credentials/signer once per handler and constructs signed or anonymous requests via an owned HTTP handler. Its close is inherited no-op while the separately constructed inner HTTP handler is held through its bound method; there is no forwarding of close in this source. This is a cleanup ownership omission to validate, not an observed connection leak. Tests use a mock transport and published example signing vectors with fixed date, not live AWS credentials, account provisioning, permission validation or bucket writes. HTTPS is default, but explicit legacy is_secure=False still chooses HTTP with a warning in the pinned code and its test. No AWS request or package was run.

FTP credentials come from trusted per-request metadata or handler defaults. Twisted FTP creates a fresh client per request, with cleanup finally beginning after data-protocol construction; local output-open failure before that try can miss client cleanup (source hypothesis). Async FTP uses per-request clients and finally closes, with transfer/data channels delegated to aioftp. TLS, proxy support and error mapping differ: async FTP supports optional SOCKS, rejects HTTP proxies, and logs/ignores per-request active-mode selection while rejecting disabled passive mode at construction. The reviewed tests include local failure/partial-body/credential fixtures and conditional dependency skips, not fleet identity isolation or runtime results here. Byte writes precede maxsize checks; warnings do not enforce limits and per-request zero disables them. Unsupported-proxy error formatting includes the reconstructed credential-bearing proxy URL, a possible secret-in-error path to handle in owned logging; no real credential was supplied or exposed.

Combined handler tests verify named-handler metadata overrides scheme selection and temporary body-file cleanup on ordinary failure, while StopDownload intentionally preserves partial output. Thus cleanup ownership and retained artifacts depend on the exact failure category, not simply whether the request succeeded. Completed exact FTP mockserver (93): it binds loopback, grants a temporary anonymous root broad FTP permissions, spawns a Python process, and removes the directory before killing/communicating with that process. It has no windowsHide or bounded startup-read timeout in this wrapper, so it is not directly reusable as this PC's isolated hidden-process harness. Cleanup failure during rmtree could prevent subsequent process cleanup; this is source ordering, not an observed leftover. No mock server was launched. No production settings, cloud accounts, downloads or local data files were changed.


## Referrer privacy and trusted policy selection

Completed referrer middleware/tests (445/1147) and URL helpers/tests (207/499). Policy selection prioritizes trusted request metadata, then recognized response policy names, then settings; response headers cannot supply Python import paths, and a dedicated async-output test asserts warning plus fallback for such a path. Settings/metadata may load trusted custom classes. Comma lists use the last recognized name; unknown metadata bypasses response-policy selection and falls back to settings. These are policy selection semantics, not authentication of the server response or account.

The stored default is the Scrapy-specific downgrade policy, with additional local file/S3 source exclusions; upstream comments about browser defaults are not evidence of current browser behavior. Depending on selected policy, cross-origin forwarding can include the source path/query. strip_url removes credentials/fragment by default but retains query unless origin_only is chosen, as explicit URL tests show. A no-referrer result does not clear a manually preexisting Referer header: get_processed_request only adds with setdefault when a value exists. Thus this middleware supplies defaults rather than sanitizing arbitrary caller-supplied headers; dedicated tests mainly start with empty headers and are not an enforced DLP guarantee. Referer is request-derived context, not an identity credential.

URL helper domain matching uses lowercased netloc rather than the offsite middleware hostname: the tests explicitly require a supplied port for an IP URL containing that port. These two helpers are not interchangeable authorization predicates. File-path guessing can choose file URIs and tests include Windows/UNC/alternate-stream syntax recognition; recognition is not confinement or permission to read a path. Redirect normalization delegates to w3lib and preserves selected fragments; it is not a network allowlist. No policy code, URL request, browser or file handler was executed.


## Crawler offsite policy is not an egress sandbox

Completed offsite downloader middleware/tests (135/313). It attaches to request_scheduled and also runs as downloader middleware, raising IgnoreRequest for disallowed URL hostnames. Empty/missing allowed_domains permits all; configured domain matches include its subdomains and ignore URL port. Trusted dont_filter/allow_offsite metadata can exempt a request, and allowed_domains mutation refreshes the cached regex. Tests cover both scheduled/direct middleware methods, suffix boundaries, dynamic changes, exemption flags and per-domain versus per-request counts. They do not perform DNS or establish network-address confinement.

This checks parsed hostname syntax, not resolved IP/subnet, DNS rebinding, protocol capability or account authorization. It is therefore a crawl-scope policy for trusted spider code rather than an outbound sandbox for untrusted agents. Logging once per domain does not stop filtering subsequent requests: tests assert one domain count but two filtered requests. The domains_seen set has no local cardinality budget, separate from request/network quotas. No external domain, DNS query, offsite traffic or runtime test occurred.

## Start, depth, length and HTTP-status filters

Completed start implementation/test/process-start integration tests (32/42/135), depth implementation/tests (131/138), URL length implementation/tests (50/64), and HTTP-error implementation/tests (93/261). Start markers use setdefault and the test preserves caller-supplied false or arbitrary string values; this scheduling label is not authenticated origin/provenance. Start middleware tests check ordered synthetic items and successful finish through delayed generator hooks, not cancellation or an outbound request budget.

Depth filtering enforces an enabled limit only where source response depth exists; no-response start/errback outputs pass without adding depth. A trusted depth_reset is consumed and sets depth zero before checking the limit; metadata continuity is not a global work budget. Tests explicitly cover reset, no-response exemption, priority changes and first-warning suppression with continued ignored counters. URL-length filtering drops over-limit output requests and is disabled at zero, but it bounds the stored URL string in this middleware, not all dispatch paths or total work/resources.

HTTP-error filtering runs after a response already exists; it does not stop the server receiving a request. Request metadata/spider/settings can allow selected/all statuses, and the integration test intentionally parses a denied status through an errback. Other tests assert ignored counts and log-level filtering, demonstrating that lack of a captured INFO message is not lack of filtering. Error recovery and generated follow-up requests need separate quotas and scope checks. These are reviewed test oracles, not executed crawler runs, and no third-party HTTP requests occurred.


## Request metadata propagation and warning-only detection

Completed spider middleware manager (328), base/tests (112/154), metadata-copy detector/tests (66/135), and sticky metadata/tests (41/93). The detector warns once per crawl on a configured set of internal keys and always returns the request unchanged; later instances are suppressed and start/errback outputs with no response are exempt. Its name is not evidence of rejection, cleanup or isolation. It checks key presence, not provenance of an actual copy, and logs source/target URLs; evaluate log sensitivity separately from field-name warnings. Tests assert log behavior, not blocked requests.

Sticky metadata is disabled without configured keys, copies with setdefault, and shares the referenced value directly rather than deep-copying or checking destination/tenant. This can support intentional continuity but cannot enforce account/session isolation; explicit follow-up values win. The helper test runs synchronous and asynchronous paths on the same mutable request objects, so the later path can see metadata already inserted by the first; these two returned results are not fully independent mutation oracles. No cross-account transfer was reproduced.

The manager requires async output support, reverses output/exception chains relative to input, and allows exception handlers to produce new output that resumes through later layers. Already-yielded items/requests are not rolled back when iteration later fails. Completed full manager tests (538) and output-chain tests (567). Their explicit oracles preserve items yielded before generator failure, recovery can enqueue a further request, and unhandled failures reach each exception layer once. A weak-reference test disables GC while asserting the response is released after iterator deletion, giving a scoped retention oracle. Some generic success helpers check result count and only the first item type, while output-chain cases assert precise ordered processing markers and final item counts through logs. Generic Exception catches exclude cancellation BaseExceptions under ordinary Python semantics; exact utility/runtime dependencies remain separate. Completed full utility module (404), tracing _MutableAsyncChain through _async_chain/as_async_generator: extend nests the existing and appended iterators, preserving progressive yielding rather than buffering for all-or-nothing output. No explicit chain-length/resource budget, aclose API or rollback appears in this wrapper. Other helpers are scoped: binary_is_text only checks a selected control-byte set, not valid Unicode/safe content; import-path syntax checking does not authorize an imported component; no-arg memoization keys by weak object and ignores subsequently passed arguments by contract; exception-cause iteration has a cycle guard. Completed full utility tests (267): chain tests assert progressive extension and that an exception ends before later appended iterators, while conversion tests check explicit encoding/replacement, and signature cases distinguish Python versions. The binary-text test explicitly accepts UTF-16 bytes and a non-UTF-8 currency byte, reinforcing its narrow control-byte heuristic. The no-arg cache test checks same-object result reuse, without a retention, mutation or argument-bearing oracle. No helper was executed. Optional middleware registration or a warning does not establish final request rejection. No crawler callbacks or middleware were executed here.


## Crawler test execution gates and trusted loading

Completed add-on loader/tests (73/244), IDNA fallback patch (78), and root conftest (300). Add-on tests explicitly distinguish setting priorities, ordered hooks, NotConfigured disabling and a trusted fallback hook that replaces SCHEDULER even when the user supplied one, retaining the old value separately. Settings priority is a convention offered to trusted code, not a capability boundary that prevents hooks from changing execution policy. Conftest does not skip requires_internet tests: it marks them flaky with retries. Therefore any future isolated test selection must explicitly exclude those cases; marker presence alone is not a no-network guarantee. Optional reactor/dependency/MITM skips are different gates. Session fixtures can start mock services and module import generates localhost test keys outside xdist workers. This is reviewed behavior, not executed here, and no suite/import was used for inventory. The aiohttp leak fixture checks tracked sessions and captured unclosed-session logs after each test, providing a targeted resource oracle but not proof all transports are closed.

Add-ons are trusted Python imports and arbitrary pre-crawler/settings hooks, with NotConfigured caught only in the ordinary loading path. No sandbox or per-worker permission enforcement follows from this loader. IDNA fallback patches selected Twisted module references process-wide when they still match captured originals; it falls back to stdlib IDNA/ASCII and restricts output character syntax. It is compatibility normalization, not authenticated DNS, destination authorization or a confusable-account defense. Exact Twisted/idna/stdlib behavior remains a versioned dependency. No host settings, DNS, keys or networking were changed.


## Scheduling fairness versus authenticated quotas

Completed priority-queue tests (533) and MockDownloader helper (36), against previously complete pqueues implementation. Oracles cover empty/failed/dropping queues, start-request versus regular priority, persistence reopen, slot cleanup, rotating ties and preference for fewer active downloads. The fairness tests use a mock active-list count and manually selected slot metadata, not real sockets, distributed queue contention or an authenticated per-account quota. Two equal-score slots alternate in the fixture; this is not a general latency/starvation guarantee under changing load.

Default start/non-start FIFO/LIFO choices and priority are scheduling policy; a request-controlled slot label is not a verified account/device/IP. Slot directory deletion is an asserted local cleanup after drain, not secure erasure of retained request state or proof that previously dispatched effects ceased. Disk ordering/reopen cases are separate from crash durability and lease-based external effects. No queue tests were executed or external crawl performed.

## Crawler queue serialization and durability oracles

Completed queue tests (274) and request queue tests (272), rereading queue wrappers (202). Tests cover FIFO/LIFO ordering, peek, repeated headers, serializable objects, recursive request identity, unsupported peek and explicit nonserializable failures. Most disk/SQLite lifecycle tests are inherited from external queuelib.tests.test_queue, which is not part of the pinned Scrapy file inventory; reading these subclasses is not reading that external test implementation. The SQLite test named survives_unclean_shutdown closes the connection directly without the queue close hook, reopens it and reads one request. It is an explicit persistence oracle, but not a process kill, power-loss, multi-worker contention or external effect exactly-once test. It was not run here.

Wrappers deserialize after the underlying pop and reconstruct a request afterward, with no lease/ack/requeue step in this wrapper; failures at those stages are not automatically retried here. Exact storage atomicity remains the queuelib dependency. Pickle loaders intentionally restore Python objects and therefore require trusted local queue state, not untrusted fleet enrollment/messages. Serialized request metadata and headers are operational data, not encrypted vault storage or per-worker authorization. Memory queues retain live request objects, while disk wrappers reconstruct via the crawler spider; ordering/persistence does not imply isolated account identity or cleanup of saved credentials. No queue bytes, credentials or external requests were executed.


## Robots parser backend and version oracles

Completed parser interface tests (235), optional-parser availability helper (8), and reread dependency compatibility (33). The base tests contain mutually exclusive length-precedence and order-precedence expectations; subclasses deliberately skip one according to parser/backend/version. Python wildcard/longest-match coverage is enabled by the pinned compatibility expression for Python 3.14.5+ or 3.13.14+ before 3.14, while its Sitemap test is skipped; optional Rerp is skipped if absent and skips longest-match precedence. Protego skips order precedence. These source-defined thresholds are not claims about installed versions or independently checked release status.

Empty/garbage bodies are explicitly expected to allow, decoding ignores selected invalid bytes/BOM, and crawl-delay/sitemap tests inspect returned metadata rather than prove scheduler enforcement. A Unicode user-agent string selects a policy group; it is not evidence of crawler operator identity or a trustworthy client declaration. Respecting robots remains crawler policy, not access control at a protected endpoint. Full RFC and middleware review remain the earlier evidence; backend package implementations are separate exact dependencies, and no website was fetched or parser suite executed.


## HTTP/2 pool, stream and cancellation state

Completed HTTP/2 handler (142), agent/pool (173), protocol (498), stream (534), empty package initializer (1), protocol tests (808), and handler tests (217). The pool key includes scheme/host/port/local bind address, correcting its stale three-field comment. It has no account/session/operator field: multiplexed transport sharing is not a workload authorization boundary. This handler explicitly rejects proxies and plain HTTP, and tests assert both; the inherited proxy/MITM classes are skipped. It does not provide proxy rotation merely because other handlers do.

Connection-pending cancellation removes that waiting Deferred from its queue but does not cancel the separate endpoint connection attempt. close_connections aborts only already pooled connections, has no closed latch and does not cancel pending connects or await connectionLost. A connection completing after that call is therefore a source-level lifecycle gap to test, not an observed surviving socket. Old-connection removal is identity-checked and has a targeted test that preserves a newer connection.

At the established protocol layer, streams wait FIFO for negotiated concurrency. Cancellation of a not-yet-sent stream calls Stream.close, which calls protocol.pop_stream; pop_stream decrements active_streams and attempts pending sends without removing that stream from the pending deque. The close then sets flags and delivers a local response (499 if no status). This exposes a source hypothesis for a canceled queued stream being initiated later and active accounting dropping below its true value. The direct cancellation test only asserts local 499; the 500-request success test and inactive-connection-loss test are separate scenarios and do not establish no transmission after queued cancellation. No canceled-request traffic or package runtime was executed here.

Data is buffered before stop/size checks; flow-controlled length, including protocol padding, feeds both quota accounting and a cumulative acknowledge_received_data call. Whether cumulative acknowledgment miscredits the hyper-h2 window requires review of the exact dependency and a synthetic oracle; no protocol violation is claimed. StopDownload retains partial body, ordinary reset clears it, and data-loss acceptance is separately configured. A test named concurrent_requests_same_domain awaits the requests sequentially, whereas the protocol x10/500 helpers actually await a DeferredList of concurrent work. Stream identifiers, peer TLS certificate and response peer address are transport facts, not authenticated crawler operator/account identities.


## Alternate crawler transport boundaries

Completed URL parse cache/helper tests (67/68), experimental aiohttp handler/test declarations (169/118), experimental httpx handler (250) and common streaming base (388). URL parse results are shared by exact URL across live request/response holders plus a 1024-entry string cache; this caches parsing, not authorization or tenant data. Aiohttp uses DummyCookieJar and httpx uses NullCookieJar, leaving application-cookie policy to the already reviewed Scrapy middleware. The exact helper and inherited test base were already fully read earlier and were rechecked here at _download_handlers.py 1–178 and download_handlers_http.py 717–745, 1716–1935. NullCookieJar overrides extraction and insertion with no-ops. The inherited cookie oracle asserts a Set-Cookie response does not create a later Cookie request header; this is stronger than a declaration, but remains unexecuted here.

Both streaming handlers strip Proxy-Authorization from prepared origin headers and reconstruct gateway credentials separately. httpx caches an entire client per credential-bearing proxy URL with no eviction until close; this is connection reuse state, not an account/session boundary. Per-client pool limits are not themselves an aggregate limit across that dictionary; the earlier outer downloader budget is a separate enforcement layer. Client-close operations are sequential; a default-client close error prevents later proxy-client closes in this method. No close failure, connection growth or credential leak was reproduced.

The streaming base opens body_file in write mode before the headers_received stop hook and writes each chunk before stop/maxsize decisions. A header stop can therefore follow a local file truncation, and a chunk can transiently exceed the size budget before truncate/cancel. These observations distinguish cancellation from undo and streaming acceptance from peak allocation limits; no file download was executed. Bindaddress request metadata logs and is ignored by these handlers, while handler-level bind address goes to the transport. Response ip_address is derived from transport peer/server metadata, not the caller observed egress or authenticated client/device. aiohttp test subclasses inherit most tests and include an explicitly internet-marked class; no test suite or external test was run. Completed the httpx-specific test file (266): it asserts pool configuration values, unsupported proxies/non-Basic header failure and optional HTTP/2 behavior. The inherited proxy oracle checks stalled CONNECT closes its local proxy connection and a separate MITM-marked test asserts no Proxy-Authorization at the destination. These are synthetic mock/proxy test designs, not runtime results from this session. Optional HTTP/2/SOCKS/IDNA conditions skip or alter portions, and source declarations do not guarantee every environment runs them.

## Crawler proxy credentials versus account identity

Completed HTTP proxy middleware tests (457) and reread middleware (103). Environment proxy routes are captured at middleware construction; per-request metadata takes precedence, including explicit no-proxy selection, and environment bypass checks are not a universal outbound access-control policy. The middleware parses a configured gateway and prepares request metadata/Basic proxy authorization; it neither allocates IP addresses nor proves the eventual observed egress, device or application account. No proxy or network request was created.

Tests directly assert credential replacement, removal when gateway changes or is disabled, and preservation when a credential-free URL still names the same gateway. Removing user information from that same URL therefore does not by itself revoke the existing Proxy-Authorization header; the explicit test expects preservation. These are request-object mutation oracles, not wire-level confidentiality tests. An owned worker/account assignment must keep proxy credentials, origin authorization, cookies and request metadata in independently scoped state and revoke each relevant binding; changing a gateway is not a new authenticated account/session or an anonymity guarantee. The code trusts supplied metadata and manually supplied headers and contains no per-worker RBAC/vault or account provisioning. Alternate handlers/CONNECT forwarding remain separate paths to trace. Tests were read, not executed; no credentials or external infrastructure were used.


## Transformation design claims versus executable coverage

Completed parity-even/odd/zero corpora (31 each), SQL hex corpus (10), and six transformation ADRs (67/83/65/76/65/137). Rechecked registration (66) and searched the pinned tree: the three parity names and sqlHexDecode occur only in their data fixtures, with no default implementation registration. Under the reviewed shared runner those cases silently return, so they cannot support default parity/SQL-hex feature claims. All inventoried transformation corpora are now read; this is not a claim that they were run or that external plugin registration is impossible.

The ADRs are upstream retrospective design narratives, not independently verified PR evidence or measurements. Hex ADR itself acknowledges chosen best-effort discussion differs from shipped strict helper behavior. Cache ADR states value-pointer/length/prefix keys and per-phase release, consistent with the prior rule/transaction review, and explicitly leaves pooled map high-water capacity unbounded. Lower duplicate entries is not a memory budget or benchmark result established here. Cache correctness assumes transformations depend only on their input; the trusted plugin registry accepts arbitrary callbacks and does not enforce purity. No cross-policy plugin mutation or stale result was reproduced, so this is a documented contract assumption, separate from the already established name-only dataset memoization omission.

## Remaining transformation corpus semantics

Completed cmdLine/compressWhitespace/CSS/generic-escape/JS corpora (46/45/45/115/108), comment removal/marker removal/replacement (150/149/150), length/lowercase/uppercase (31/38/38), null removal/replacement (59/52), whitespace removal (38), trim/left/right (66 each), URL Unicode decoding/encoding (255/143) and UTF-8 representation (52). These literal-data fixtures corroborate byte mappings, comment-only NUL output and distinct removal/replacement semantics. They do not execute interpreted content or establish real browser/application equivalence. Several ret values disagree with whether output changed (including uppercase and URL cases), explaining why the generic runner ignores them; dedicated changed-flag tests remain the relevant oracle.

The UTF-8 corpus contains mojibake-looking character sequences and corresponding encodings of those literal code points; it must not be described as clean real-language input coverage simply because the file is long. The dedicated Go test includes direct Japanese and supplementary-character samples. URL corpora cover the full byte domain, selected best-fit characters, malformed percent sequences and fullwidth fallback, but not every Unicode code point or signed account-identifier normalization.
